The next lemma shows that continuous maps from function space to weight space can be realized, on the relevant set, by continuous equivariant maps that operate purely in weight space.

Lemma F.16
.

Let
A=({\bm{d}},\sigma)
be an architecture with corresponding weight space
\mathcal{V}_{A}
, symmetry group
G_{A}
and exclusion set
{\mathcal{E}}_{A}
(see Definition
3.4
), and let

\Psi:{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)\to\mathcal{V}_{A}
be continuous. Then, for any compact set
K^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{A}\setminus{\mathcal{E}}_{A}
,
there exists a continuous map

\Psi^{*}:\bigcup_{g\in G}(g\cdot K^{*})\to\mathcal{V}_{A}

(269)

such that the following holds for all
{\bm{v}}\in\bigcup_{g\in G}\rho(g)K^{*}
:

1.

Equivariance:

\Psi^{*}(\rho(g){\bm{v}})=\rho(g)\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}})\quad\forall g\in G_{A}.

(270)

2.

Functional equality:

f_{\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}})}=f_{\Psi({\bm{v}})}.

(271)

Proof.

For simplicity, assume without loss of generality that
K^{*}=\bigcup_{g\in G}\rho(g)K^{*}
. By Lemma
F.6
, there exists a continuous map
\mathrm{cannon}:K^{*}\to K^{*}
such that for every
{\bm{v}}\in K^{*}
and every
g\in G
:

\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})=\mathrm{cannon}(\rho(g){\bm{v}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})\in\mathrm{orbit}({\bm{v}}).

(272)

Moreover, when constructing
\mathrm{cannon}
, we arranged that, for each
{\bm{v}}\in K^{*}
, there exists a
unique

g_{\bm{v}}\in G
such that

\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})=\rho(g_{\bm{v}}){\bm{v}}.

(273)

For any
h\in G
and
{\bm{v}}^{\prime}:=h\cdot{\bm{v}}
, we thus have

\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})=\rho(g_{{\bm{v}}^{\prime}}){\bm{v}}^{\prime}=\rho(g_{{\bm{v}}^{\prime}})\rho(h){\bm{v}}.

(274)

From the uniqueness of
g_{\bm{v}}
we thus have

g_{\rho(h){\bm{v}}}=g_{\bm{v}}h^{-1}.

(275)

Define
\Psi^{*}:K^{*}\to\mathcal{V}_{A}
by

\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}}):=\rho((g_{\bm{v}})^{-1})\Psi\bigl(f_{\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})}\bigr).

(276)

This is well-defined because
g_{\bm{v}}\in G
is uniquely determined by
{\bm{v}}
. Additionally, as the maps
v\to\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})
,
{\bm{v}}\to f_{\bm{v}}
(where continuity follows from Proposition
C.2
) and
{\bm{v}}\to g_{\bm{v}}
are all continuous on
K^{*}
,
\Psi^{*}
is also continuous. Moreover, since
\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})\in\mathrm{orbit}({\bm{v}})
, we have
f_{\bm{v}}=f_{\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})}
and so

\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}})=\rho((g_{\bm{v}})^{-1})\Psi(f_{\bm{v}}).

(277)

Since
\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}})\in\mathrm{orbit}(\Psi(f_{\bm{v}}))
, functional equality holds. Finally, from equation
275
we have

\Psi^{*}(\rho(h){\bm{v}})=\rho((g_{\rho(h){\bm{v}}})^{-1})\Psi\bigl(f_{\mathrm{cannon}(\rho(h){\bm{v}})}\bigr)=\rho((g_{\bm{v}}h^{-1})^{-1})\Psi\bigl(f_{\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})}\bigr)=\rho(h)\rho(g_{\bm{v}})^{-1}\Psi\bigl(f_{\mathrm{cannon}({\bm{v}})}\bigr)=\rho(h)\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}}).

(278)

which is exactly the equivariance property, completing the proof.

∎

We are now ready for our final proof.

Proof of Theorem
F.10
.

First, since
\Psi
is continuous and
K
is compact, the image
\Psi(K)
is compact as well. Additionally, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that

\Psi\!\big(B_{\delta}(K)\big)\subseteq B_{\epsilon/4}\!\big(\Psi(K)\big).

(279)

Now, Lemma
F.15
states there exists a continuous map
I:B_{\epsilon/4}\!\big(\Psi(K)\big)\to\mathcal{V}_{A}
such that, for every
f\in B_{\epsilon/4}\!\big(\Psi(K)\big)
,

\|f-f_{I(f)}\|_{\infty}<\epsilon.

(280)

Thus, the function
I\circ\Psi:B_{\delta}(K)\to\mathcal{V}_{A}
is continuous and satisfies

\|f_{I\circ\Psi(f)}-\Psi(f)\|_{\infty}<\epsilon.

(281)

Now, from Lemma
F.16
we get that for any compact set
K^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{A}\setminus{\mathcal{E}}_{A}
that is a
\delta
approximation of
K
,
there exists a continuous equivariant map
\Psi^{*}:\bigcup_{g\in G}\rho(g)K^{*}\to\mathcal{V}_{A}
such that

f_{\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}})}=f_{I\circ\Psi(f_{\bm{v}})}\quad\forall{\bm{v}}\in\bigcup_{g\in G}\rho(g)K^{*}.

(282)

This means that for every
{\bm{v}}\in K^{*}

\|f_{\Psi^{*}({\bm{v}})}-\Psi(f_{\bm{v}})\|_{\infty}<\epsilon.

(283)

Since
\Psi^{*}
is equivariant and continuous and
K^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{A}\setminus{\mathcal{E}}_{A}
, Theorem
7.4
implies that we can approximate
\Psi^{*}
using a DWS network to any precision, completing the proof.

∎
